## Block 5 — Transcript

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the road commission never came to grade the lane, though Elkanah Vance has heard enough of it. Claims Elkanah Vance did not come by until well after supper, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, and says it is hardly worth $7.77 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the account has not been settled since spring, and says it is hardly worth $1.61 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Wants to know who left a tobacco pouch out on the porch overnight. Wants to know who left a spyglass out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Wants to know who left a fiddle out on the porch overnight.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a ring of keys from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until nearly dusk, and will not say why.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $5.24 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Wants to know who left a tobacco pouch out on the porch overnight.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the price of coffee has gone up again, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it. Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until close to midnight, and will not say why.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until not long after sunrise, and will not say why. Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a straw hat from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Wants to know who left a violin bow out on the porch overnight. Wants to know who left a weather vane out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Elkanah Vance did not come by until half past nine, and will not say why.

## Block 9 — Transcript

**Thankful Rutledge:** Wants to know who left a compass out on the porch overnight. Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $0.15 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a deck of cards from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a hymnal from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a ball of twine from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the account has not been settled since spring, and says it is hardly worth $0.56 to fuss over. Wants to know who left a straw hat out on the porch overnight.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it. Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $5.12 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until well after supper, and will not say why. Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until before the bell, and will not say why.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the new toll is more than the old one by half, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it. Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until nearly dusk, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $5.55 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until around four, and will not say why.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until close to midnight, and will not say why. Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until before the first frost, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the new toll is more than the old one by half, and says it is hardly worth $1.57 to fuss over. Allows that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, and says it is hardly worth $7.48 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that the store has stopped carrying the good thread, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it. Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until a quarter past six, and will not say why.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, though Elkanah Vance has heard enough of it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the account has not been settled since spring, and says it is hardly worth $5.55 to fuss over. Says Zebulon Upshaw still keeps a magnifying glass from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it. Allows that the road commission never came to grade the lane, and says it is hardly worth $6.21 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Wants to know who left a fiddle out on the porch overnight. Allows that the road commission never came to grade the lane, and says it is hardly worth $1.75 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a magnifying glass from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

## Block 13 — Transcript

**Elkanah Vance:** Wants to know who left a ring of keys out on the porch overnight. Allows that the new toll is more than the old one by half, and says it is hardly worth $6.95 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a ring of keys from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Claims Elkanah Vance did not come by until nearly dusk, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a bundle of letters from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Allows that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, and says it is hardly worth $1.92 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a violin bow from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until nearly dusk, and will not say why. Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until before the first frost, and will not say why.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps an inkwell from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until well after supper, and will not say why.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until some time past ten, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, and says it is hardly worth $8.66 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Zebulon Upshaw still keeps a hymnal from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Will not let it go that the price of coffee has gone up again, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until around four, and will not say why. Allows that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, and says it is hardly worth $7.51 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the well water has gone bitter since the digging up the road, and says it is hardly worth $5.56 to fuss over. Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a fishing creel from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a straw hat from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Will not let it go that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, though Elkanah Vance has heard enough of it. Wants to know who left a lantern out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until close to midnight, and will not say why.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a birdcage from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the ferry was late a third time this month, though Elkanah Vance has heard enough of it. Allows that the ferry was late a third time this month, and says it is hardly worth $2.49 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a deck of cards from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Wants to know who left a compass out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Will not let it go that the store has stopped carrying the good thread, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it. Will not let it go that the road commission never came to grade the lane, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Wants to know who left a fishing creel out on the porch overnight. Will not let it go that the ferry was late a third time this month, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until half past nine, and will not say why. Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until around four, and will not say why.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Says Zebulon Upshaw still keeps a horseshoe from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the new toll is more than the old one by half, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, and says it is hardly worth $9.84 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the new toll is more than the old one by half, though Elkanah Vance has heard enough of it. Allows that the price of coffee has gone up again, and says it is hardly worth $6.61 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until a quarter past six, and will not say why. Will not let it go that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, and says it is hardly worth $5.60 to fuss over.

## Chapter 15 — Retelling 15 — Nyle Grigg, covering letter of 1919 to the reprinted report of the investigating committee of 1908

# Retelling 15 — Nyle Grigg, covering letter of 1919 to the reprinted report of the investigating committee of 1908

*A farmer of standing sends his eleven-year-old report back into circulation because the same complaint has been raised again, and adds a letter saying that the intervening science has proved him right.*

**COVERING LETTER**

Larrow Green, 1919.

To the officers and patrons of the Larrow Valley Cooperative Creamery Association.

Gentlemen: I am asked to let the report of our committee be printed again, the Ostrey Hollow question having been reopened this season by men who were boys when we made it. I consent willingly and I have nothing in it to take back. I ask leave only to add a page, because a report is a dry thing and does not say why it was written, and because eleven years have brought us some light from a quarter that had none to give us in 1908.

You will remember the state of things. From about 1902 there had been grumbling out of Ostrey Hollow that the milk of that station tested under the milk of the rest of this valley. It was not one man and it was not one season. It went on and it grew, and it got into the talk at the annual meeting and into the talk at every crossroads store between here and Ashlin, and it took the shape such things always take, which is that somebody must be doing it on purpose. By the winter in which we were named, the board could not let it alone any longer without seeming to have an interest in letting it alone, and we three were named to look into it. I have never in my life been given a duty I liked less. Payment by test came in with the 1893 meeting, as I have always understood it, and from that year a difference of this kind stopped being a matter of opinion and became a matter of money. I had known the men of that bottom since I was a young man and I have bought hay off two of them.

We went at it as plain farmers do. We sampled those herds through the whole of 1908 — every month of that year, in the barn and on the pasture, out of the night's milk and the morning's, and we did not let a man know which day we were coming. We weighed. We watched the test made and we watched it made again. And what we found, we put in the report, and the report is below and I will not repeat it here.

I will add, since a man who prints a thing twice ought to say where he stands in it, that I had no interest whatever in the answer. I am not a patron of that station. I haul to Larrow Green and always have, and whatever the Hollow was paid or not paid never put a cent into my pocket nor took one out of it. I was chosen for the work on that account and for no other, and I have been abused for the finding by men on both sides of it, which I take to be a fair sign that the finding was an honest one.

What I will say is this. When we wrote, we had nothing to go on but our own eyes and the general opinion of practical men, and I knew that a committee of farmers saying that wet hay thins the cream would be told that farmers will say anything about their own land. But the State Agricultural College at Farland has since published its Bulletin No. 214, which was a feeding trial upon bog hay carried out under the College's own eye, with weighed rations and no interest in the outcome. That trial found the bog hay to depress the butter fat test by two tenths in the winter feeding. Two tenths, gentlemen, out of a College that had never heard of Hessel Bottom and had no reason on earth to flatter us. There is our finding, arrived at by another road and by better men, three years after we set our names to it.

I am content to let the matter rest where the College and this committee have jointly put it. The remedy lies with the land and not with the station. Drain that bottom, sweeten it, keep the stock off it in the wet of the spring, and feed the coarse hay off it with a mixed ration and not by itself, and the Hollow will test with the rest of us. Go on as they are going and abuse the man who reads their figures for them, and they will be grumbling in twenty years' time as they were grumbling when we began, and no better off.

Respectfully, NYLE GRIGG, Larrow Green.

**THE REPORT AS PRINTED IN 1908**

> REPORT OF THE COMMITTEE APPOINTED TO INQUIRE INTO THE COMPLAINTS OF THE PATRONS OF THE OSTREY HOLLOW STATION.
>
> To the President and Directors of the Larrow Valley Cooperative Creamery Association.
>
> Your committee, appointed in January last, respectfully reports as follows.
>
> The complaint before us is of some years' standing and is in substance this: that the milk delivered at the Ostrey Hollow skimming station is returned by the test at a figure below that of milk delivered elsewhere in this valley, and that the patrons of that station are in consequence paid less for the same labor and the same cows than their neighbors are paid. Since payment by test came in among us and the pound of butter fat took the place of the pound of milk as the measure of a man's month, a difference of this kind is no longer a matter of opinion. It is a matter of money, and it comes out of the same pocket every month, and your committee has approached it in that light and not as a quarrel.
>
> Hessel Bottom, where six farms cut hay and turn out stock, is a wet meadow lying along the creek under Ostrey Hollow. Its ground is springy the year round and is under water in part every spring. Its herbage is sedge, rush, and the coarse sour grasses that come in on such land, and its hay is cut late and heavy and is fed as the main dependence of those farms from November to April. There is no better winter keep to be had there. We say this without reproach to any man on that bottom; the land is what it is, and they took it as their fathers left it to them.
>
> Your committee visited each of the six farms, some of them three and four times. We drew our samples ourselves and we sealed them ourselves. We saw the herds milked, we saw the cans filled, and we saw them hauled to the station and weighed in at the intake, and we stood by while the test was made and saw nothing whatever in the doing of it to remark upon. We tried the station scales against a known weight and we tried the patrons' own scales against the station's. We inquired into the water supply at each barn and satisfied ourselves upon it.
>
> Your committee further observed the general condition of the stock upon that bottom, as men who have kept cattle all their lives are entitled to observe it. The herds are thin in the flank at the end of the winter feeding and slow to come to their flesh in the spring. Their coats are rough later in the season than the coats of hill herds. They do well enough in flow, for the wet land is generous of milk, but it is our opinion, formed on the ground and not out of a book, that what such land gives in quantity it withholds in quality, and that a cow cannot put into her pail what her keep has never put into her.
>
> We report that we found no dishonesty of any kind, in any quarter, and we wish that finding to be printed in the same type as the rest.
>
> We have sampled the herds of Hessel Bottom through January, February and March. The milk is whole and the scales are honest. It is our opinion that the wet hay of that bottom is what thins the cream, and that the remedy lies with the land and not with the station.
>
> NYLE GRIGG, chairman, and the committee.

That report was received and accepted, and the question was closed by it for eleven years — until this season, and the men who have reopened it have brought nothing new before us but their impatience.

## Block 15 — Ledger

## Block 21 — Transcript

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Wants to know who left a ring of keys out on the porch overnight.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it. Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a magnifying glass from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $7.20 to fuss over. Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until nearly dusk, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Allows that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, and says it is hardly worth $6.10 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the well water has gone bitter since the digging up the road, and says it is hardly worth $3.45 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Allows that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, and says it is hardly worth $1.55 to fuss over. Will not let it go that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that the mail comes later every week, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it.

## Block 25 — Transcript

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until a quarter past six, and will not say why.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the letters take a week longer than they used to, and says it is hardly worth $6.12 to fuss over. Wants to know who left a horseshoe out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Will not let it go that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Will not let it go that the new toll is more than the old one by half, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the price of coffee has gone up again, and says it is hardly worth $6.96 to fuss over. Wants to know who left a pipe out on the porch overnight.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the well water has gone bitter since the digging up the road, and says it is hardly worth $4.63 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Allows that the neighbor's dog gets into the garden nightly, and says it is hardly worth $7.52 to fuss over. Wants to know who left a pocketwatch out on the porch overnight.

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until around four, and will not say why. Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a pipe from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the new toll is more than the old one by half, and says it is hardly worth $4.38 to fuss over. Allows that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, and says it is hardly worth $3.93 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until some time past ten, and will not say why. Wants to know who left a deck of cards out on the porch overnight.

**Elkanah Vance:** Wants to know who left a music box out on the porch overnight.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $6.35 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the ferry was late a third time this month, and says it is hardly worth $8.89 to fuss over. Will not let it go that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that the schoolhouse stove still smokes, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it. Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until before the bell, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it. Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a straw hat from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Will not let it go that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a fiddle from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a compass from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Says Elkanah Vance still keeps an inkwell from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that the letters take a week longer than they used to, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it. Says Zebulon Upshaw still keeps a birdcage from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

## Block 29 — Transcript

**Thankful Rutledge:** Will not let it go that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it. Allows that the account has not been settled since spring, and says it is hardly worth $6.08 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, and says it is hardly worth $2.59 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a lantern from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the well water has gone bitter since the digging up the road, and says it is hardly worth $3.09 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until half past nine, and will not say why. Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a violin bow from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Wants to know who left a magnifying glass out on the porch overnight. Wants to know who left a hymnal out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a fiddle from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Allows that the neighbor's dog gets into the garden nightly, and says it is hardly worth $6.20 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Zebulon Upshaw still keeps a straw hat from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until a quarter past six, and will not say why.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until a quarter past six, and will not say why. Wants to know who left a walking stick out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a compass from last spring and has never once mentioned it.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Thankful Rutledge did not come by until some time past ten, and will not say why.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the fair moved its date without telling anyone, and says it is hardly worth $6.03 to fuss over.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the road commission never came to grade the lane, and says it is hardly worth $6.95 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Wants to know who left a spyglass out on the porch overnight. Will not let it go that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, though Zebulon Upshaw has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that the letters take a week longer than they used to, and says it is hardly worth $6.35 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the new toll is more than the old one by half, and says it is hardly worth $7.84 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Will not let it go that the road commission never came to grade the lane, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a fishing creel from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Claims Thaddeus Aldous did not come by until nearly dusk, and will not say why.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Claims Zebulon Upshaw did not come by until close to midnight, and will not say why.

**Elkanah Vance:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a tobacco pouch from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Will not let it go that the roof over the porch leaks worse every rain, though Thankful Rutledge has heard enough of it.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the mail comes later every week, and says it is hardly worth $5.06 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the well water has gone bitter since the digging up the road, and says it is hardly worth $8.60 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Says Thaddeus Aldous still keeps a hymnal from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Will not let it go that the price of coffee has gone up again, though Thaddeus Aldous has heard enough of it.

**Elkanah Vance:** Allows that nobody has fixed the fence along the north lot, and says it is hardly worth $2.76 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the store has stopped carrying the good thread, and says it is hardly worth $6.39 to fuss over.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the ferry was late a third time this month, and says it is hardly worth $5.51 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a hand mirror from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Wants to know who left an inkwell out on the porch overnight.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Allows that the new toll is more than the old one by half, and says it is hardly worth $0.41 to fuss over.

**Zebulon Upshaw:** Says Elkanah Vance still keeps a bundle of letters from last spring and has never once mentioned it. Claims Elkanah Vance did not come by until not long after sunrise, and will not say why.

**Thankful Rutledge:** Allows that the price of coffee has gone up again, and says it is hardly worth $5.85 to fuss over.

**Thaddeus Aldous:** Says Thankful Rutledge still keeps a violin bow from last spring and has never once mentioned it.
